[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 27.9 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"4e056d","wall_time_seconds":0.8218076,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":3036,"output":"# Four Cs rubric v2\\r\\n\\r\\n## Scoring rules\\r\\n\\r\\nThere are five criteria per C, each worth 5 points. Use **0, 1, 3, or 5 only**: select the highest anchor fully supported by evidence. **0** means absent, contradicted, or not evidenced at even the 1-point level. Higher anchors retain the substantive checks below them; numerical coverage bands (such as N1) are alternatives, not simultaneous requirements. Never interpolate, start with full points and deduct, award points for irrelevant infrastructure, or replace missing evidence with optimism.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor multi-part anchors, all parts are required. A bounded sample can establish the stated sample criterion, but does not prove whole-system coverage. Name the sample and unresolved scope. If no relevant workflows or connections can be identified, those evidence-dependent criteria are 0, not vacuously complete. Documentation may earn 1 where specified; it is not a working result.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Context: can a fresh session understand and retrieve? (25)\\r\\n\\r\\n| ID | Criterion | 1 point | 3 points | 5 points |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| C1 | Personalized operating context | Non-placeholder identity and purpose exist | Identity, audience/stakeholders, priorities, and collaboration preferences have specific usable sources | All of those are consistent across the sampled current sources, with meaningful priorities and clear completion expectations |\\r\\n| C2 | Routing and findability | A manual or equivalent gives at least one specific usable route | At least 3 of the 5 probes succeed through declared routes, and the important project/knowledge indexes exist where needed | All 5 succeed through declared routes; immediate-folder comparison finds no unexplained important omissions or broken sampled routes |\\r\\n| C3 | Freshness | Changing facts have source/as-of dates | Sampled changing facts have appropriate refresh rules and current evidence, or are explicitly historical with a working current-source route | All sampled current facts are verified within their expected interval; optional caches are consistent, maintained, and optional, or absent |\\r\\n| C4 | Source authority | At least one canonical source is identified | Rules distinguish stable context, current status/metrics, and original records; sampled claims link to supporting sources | All sampled authority chains resolve; duplicates have explicit roles, and no unresolved material source/manual conflicts remain |\\r\\n| C5 | Continuity | An actual decision or project-state record exists | The sampled active project has a current deliverable, decision rationale, and next step accessible from its entry point | A second relevant project or knowledge thread can also be resumed from durable records, with current status and useful source links |\\r\\n\\r\\nA hot cache, particular wiki layout, memory folder, long manual, or extra project count is not required. For a genuinely single-project setup, C5\'s second thread can be a distinct prior decision within that project.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Connections: can it reach the information it needs? (25)\\r\\n\\r\\nInventory applicable domains before scoring. Mark irrelevant domains with a reason grounded in the user\'s role/context. Do not reward connecting tools the user does not need.\\r\\n\\r\\n| ID | Criterion | 1 point | 3 points | 5 points |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| N1 | Relevant domain access | At least one relevant domain has successful-read evidence, but fewer than half do | At least half, but not all, applicable domains have successful-read evidence | Every applicable domain has successful-read evidence within its expected interval |\\r\\n| N2 | Useful retrieval | A specific query and expected answer are documented | One actual priority-related query returns the right record, date range, and source | Two distinct priority-related queries return adequate results, with pagination/completeness or documented export limits checked |\\r\\n| N3 | Reproducible access routes | One connection has a tool/script path and purpose documented | All applicable domains have a documented route or explicit missing-connection status, and connected routes include safe auth instructions and query examples | The documented sampled access route is successfully reproduced without relying on hidden chat context; all connected domains have usable guides |\\r\\n| N4 | Appropriate action boundaries | Required read/write scopes and approval boundaries are documented | For a needed write workflow, a prior authorized successful write or faithful sandbox result is recorded; alternatively, the user\'s deliberately read-only scope is documented and verified | Sampled operations respect intended permissions and have verified failure/duplicate safeguards; do not perform a live write just to earn points |\\r\\n| N5 | Freshness and failure visibility | Source timestamps and freshness expectations are documented | Sampled reads/exports are within those expectations and failures are surfaced rather than replaced by unlabeled old data | Every applicable domain has recent success evidence plus demonstrated expired-auth/stale-data handling for at least one representative connection |\\r\\n\\r\\nN1: if there is exactly one applicable domain, its successful read meets the 5-point coverage anchor, not the other criteria automatically. Local files and exports can satisfy relevant domains when they are the real source and sufficiently current. No hidden bonus for MCPs, API keys, provider count, or write access the user does not need.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Capabilities: do relevant workflows produce usable results? (25)\\r\\n\\r\\nUse up to three priority workflows chosen before inspecting their outputs. If only one exists, assess it honestly; do not require unnecessary skills. If an identified priority needs a missing workflow, retain that gap in the sample.\\r\\n\\r\\n| ID | Criterion | 1 point | 3 points | 5 points |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| P1 | Fit and invocation | A workflow has a clear trigger tied to a stated need | One sampled workflow has a successful invocation with the intended inputs | All sampled priority workflows have successful invocation evidence, with clear boundaries between overlapping triggers |\\r\\n| P2 | Output quality | An example output and acceptance criteria exist | One actual output was checked against meaningful acceptance criteria | All sampled workflows have usable outputs verified against their acceptance criteria, not merely self-reported completion |\\r\\n| P3 | Failure handling | Expected missing-input/error cases and side effects are documented | At least one relevant missing-input, stale-source, or dependency-failure case was tested and handled correctly | Every sampled workflow has a relevant boundary-case result and respects authorization/side-effect limits |\\r\\n| P4 | Portability and discoverability | Required files/dependencies and invocation route are documented | Sampled entry points, references, and runtime registrations resolve; generated mirrors match required transforms where used | A recorded clean-session or equivalent isolated run reproduces the sampled workflow without hidden conversation state or undocumented dependencies |\\r\\n| P5 | Repeated useful use | At least one dated real use is evidenced | One sampled workflow has two distinct successful real uses with output references | All sampled workflows have repeated successful real uses; corrections are reflected in the current workflow with subsequent validation |\\r\\n\\r\\nSkill/agent count and file recency score zero by themselves. A deterministic script or simple prompt can score as highly as a complex agent. A newly created skill cannot prove repeated real use by running synthetic tests twice.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Cadence: does useful work happen reliably over time? (25)\\r\\n\\r\\nUse actual schedules and expected due times. A manually invoked ritual is useful but is not unattended execution. Configuration alone does not establish a successful run.\\r\\n\\r\\n| ID | Criterion | 1 point | 3 points | 5 points |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| D1 | Real trigger | An explicit human ritual or actual scheduler/trigger configuration exists | At least one enabled trigger has a verified runtime/host and expected output | That trigger has demonstrably run in its intended unattended environment, including runtime availability requirements |\\r\\n| D2 | Due executions | A dated manual completion or incomplete/failed automatic attempt is recorded | One due automatic execution completed with its expected output | At least two distinct due automatic executions completed; no unexplained missed due runs remain in the inspected period |\\r\\n| D3 | Observability | Run status/logging and failure-notification behavior are documented | Inspected executions have durable timestamps, outcomes, and a verified failure-reporting path | A real or safely simulated failure reached the intended reporting mechanism, and recovery is evidenced |\\r\\n| D4 | Control and recovery | Stop/disable, ownership, permissions, and recovery instructions exist | Configuration confirms those controls plus protection against inappropriate duplicate/overlapping actions | A prior or safe isolated test demonstrates stop/recovery and duplicate prevention without unintended side effects |\\r\\n| D5 | Maintenance loop | Review frequency and freshness/cleanup responsibilities are defined | One completed review repaired a real issue or verified no action was needed, with evidence | Two distinct scheduled review cycles are recorded, with follow-up verification of any fixes and maintained routing/context |\\r\\n\\r\\nDo not trigger jobs, change schedules, send test alerts, or disable automation during a read-only audit. Use prior evidence or label the test unverified. A manual-only system has a maximum **10/25 for Cadence**, even if its review discipline is strong. A newly enabled routine must wait for due-run evidence; back-to-back manual test runs are not two due executions.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Gates and stages\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Sum criterion points within each C. If the five retrieval probes cannot recover either the user\'s main purpose or any authoritative priority/status source, **Context is capped at 10**. Show its raw subtotal and the cap.\\r\\n2. Apply the manual-only Cadence cap of 10 when no enabled automatic trigger is verified. This includes a system with no triggers at all; the cap never awards points.\\r\\n3. Sum the four resulting C subtotals as the **raw total**.\\r\\n4. Apply every relevant total cap; the lowest wins:\\r\\n   - Any C below 10: total capped at **49**.\\r\\n   - Any C below 15, or fewer than two verified due automatic successes: total capped at **69**.\\r\\n   - Any C below 20, or a material unresolved routing/source-authority conflict: total capped at **84**.\\r\\n5. Final score = min(raw total, applicable caps). Report each applied cap and why; never silently alter arithmetic.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Final score | Stage |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| 0-24 | Unproven |\\r\\n| 25-49 | Foundation |\\r\\n| 50-69 | Working, with gaps |\\r\\n| 70-84 | Dependable in the verified scope |\\r\\n| 85-100 | Maintained and evidenced |\\r\\n\\r\\nNever label a high score universally autonomous, safe, or complete. State what was sampled. Unknown evidence is a verification gap, not proof that a system is broken. These gates intentionally prevent a large skill library from compensating for absent connections or cadence.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Calibration checks\\r\\n\\r\\n- Fresh template, named daily skills, API keys, and recent file timestamps: no execution/output/domain-access credit. A fresh clone should remain Unproven, not jump to 70+.\\r\\n- Personalized manual, useful references, 60 skills, and 10 agents but no observed outputs or runs: presence cannot earn full Capabilities or Cadence.\\r\\n- Excellent Context/Connections/Capabilities at 25 each and manual-only Cadence at 10: raw 85, final 69. No automatic success evidence means the 69 cap applies.\\r\\n- All Cs at 21 with two due successes and no material conflicts: raw/final 84. At 23 each under the same conditions: raw/final 92.\\r\\n- A stale required hot cache that contradicts a canonical source lowers freshness/authority credit and prevents a score above 84 until resolved. No hot cache incurs no penalty.\\r\\n- A monthly routine with two evidenced due executions can earn repeat-run credit; a newly configured daily job with no executions cannot.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"b12702","wall_time_seconds":0.6031019,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2158,"output":"# Automatic audit history\\r\\n\\r\\nRead this at the start of every AIOS audit. The dated reports are the durable history; each new report carries the finding ledger forward. Do not maintain a separate editable score database or depend on this conversation. Writing reports does not schedule future audits or repair the inspected system.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 1. Select prior evidence\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Resolve the audited project\'s root and its existing `audits/` directory. Inspect report names and metadata first, not every audit\'s full contents. The folder may also contain cost, code, or security audits: these are not AIOS baselines.\\r\\n2. Use `audit_kind: aios`, project identity, run timestamp, report status, rubric version, and coverage to select reports. For legacy reports without metadata, read their headings/scope and identify them explicitly as legacy; never guess scores, IDs, or dates from memory. Do not change historical files to match the new format.\\r\\n3. Read the latest applicable report\'s ledger, plus the most recent complete comparable report if different. Start with at most two full reports. Follow older links only for unresolved identity/evidence or older comparable scope; note limits if that is not possible. Exclude fixture/example reports. Inspect overlapping/parallel runs when present so one branch\'s unresolved findings do not disappear.\\r\\n4. Compare scores only for the same project, rubric, and materially equivalent scope: runtimes, domains, workflows, and retrieval probes. A moved checkout of the same project may qualify when identity is verified. Different projects, changed scoring anchors, narrower/broader samples, and partial reports do not support a simple score delta. Finding-level comparisons can still be valid where targets/checks match.\\r\\n5. Record the previous report link(s) and separately the score baseline, if any. No eligible saved report means **first recorded baseline**. Do not treat any unsaved audit as freshly verified evidence. Historical imports require an explicit request and a clearly labeled source/date.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 2. Maintain finding identity and status\\r\\n\\r\\nGive each new underlying finding a stable ID such as `AIOS-<run-id>-01`. Preserve the original ID on later runs, matching by affected route/workflow, runtime, and failure mode. Record verified renames/moves instead of minting duplicate findings. If identity is ambiguous, explain it before merging. Class (defect, verification gap, opportunity, intentional difference), evidence confidence, and progress status are separate fields.\\r\\n\\r\\nCarry the previous report\'s complete tracked ledger into the new report, including unresolved items outside today\'s sample and compact entries for previously closed items. Keep original first-seen and last-verified timestamps. An item omitted from the three recommended actions is not closed. Use these progress labels:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Status | Evidence required |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| New | First observed in this history. Say newly detected, not newly introduced, unless prior evidence establishes the latter. |\\r\\n| Still open | Rechecked and the defect/verification gap remains, or a tracked opportunity is confirmed pending. |\\r\\n| Resolved | The original completion check passes with current evidence. A proposed edit, changed timestamp, installed copy, or self-reported success alone does not establish resolution. |\\r\\n| Reopened | Previously resolved; the same failure is demonstrated again. Keep the ID and reference its prior closure. |\\r\\n| Not rechecked | Not covered, inaccessible, or insufficient evidence today. Preserve last verified state; never imply resolution or a new failure. |\\r\\n| No longer applicable | Explicitly changed requirements, retired workflow, or other evidenced scope change. Explain why; this is not a successful repair. |\\r\\n\\r\\nA previously resolved finding not checked today is `Not rechecked` with last verified state `Resolved`, not a reopened defect. If a prior suspected defect is disproved or found intentional, record the correction and evidence in the new report; do not call it a repair or erase the old observation.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor each tracked finding record: ID, class, affected target/runtime, first seen, prior status, current status, last verified timestamp/state, evidence link(s), and completion check. The current state is always as of this run, not guaranteed live between audits.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 3. Explain progress honestly\\r\\n\\r\\nUse a compact transition table and short narrative:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Finding ID | Prior state | This run | Evidence / practical change | Next check |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Existing stable ID | Prior recorded state | One status above | Current evidence, or reason not rechecked | Original acceptance check or justified revision |\\r\\n\\r\\n- Separate **actual fixes**, **newly verified evidence**, **regressions**, and **coverage or rubric changes**. More logs can justify more credit without any system improvement; a newly discovered defect may lower the score without a recent regression.\\r\\n- For a valid score comparison, show prior/current four subtotals, raw totals, caps, and final totals. Explain which criterion evidence changed and any cap effect. Otherwise say **scores not directly comparable** and explain why; do not show a misleading delta or trend.\\r\\n- Show unique finding counts by progress state, with rechecked versus carried-forward coverage. Treat no-longer-applicable and corrected classifications separately from verified repairs. Do not claim improvement merely because the score rose or the open count shrank.\\r\\n- Prioritize the next checks by user impact, including important unresolved prior items. Do not rerun paid workflows, send alerts, or make repairs just to earn resolution credit.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 4. Save every run safely\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Use [the report template](templates/report.md). Generate a real UTC timestamp and collision-resistant run ID, for example a timestamp plus random suffix. Default filename: `audits/audit-YYYY-MM-DD-HHMMSS-<suffix>.md`, using UTC. Include timezone-aware timestamps and the exact audited root in metadata. These are local records, not public uploads.\\r\\n2. Fill every required report section with actual evidence, or an explicit not-checked/partial explanation. Keep full criterion arithmetic, the current finding ledger, prior-report links, comparison limits, and proposed completion checks. Do not leave sample placeholders or invented results.\\r\\n3. Create the file without overwriting an existing path; use exclusive creation or equivalent collision protection, retrying with a fresh suffix if necessary. Never silently replace an earlier same-day run. Preserve parallel runs. Do not rewrite historical reports when a later run resolves an issue; later reports document the transition.\\r\\n4. Read back the saved report. Verify metadata, links to prior reports, score arithmetic, finding IDs/statuses, redaction, and that the saved content agrees with the reported result. Resolve relative links from the report\'s directory. Link the saved absolute file path in chat, with a concise progress summary.\\r\\n5. If the audit stops early, save observed evidence as `report_status: partial`, with unavailable scores set to `null`, reasons, and unchecked findings carried forward. A partial run is not a full-score baseline. Unexpected process termination may prevent saving; never claim otherwise.\\r\\n6. If the filesystem cannot be written, say **report not saved**, return the report in chat, and describe the specific blocker. Do not substitute a secret-bearing log dump, silently save elsewhere, or claim history was updated. An explicit user instruction not to save takes precedence; say **not saved at your request**.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe audited workspace remains unchanged apart from the new report and its directory. Do not add an audit entry to business memory, alter `decisions/log.md` on each audit, or update the manuals automatically. Existing reports are point-in-time evidence, never canonical current business facts.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Calibration checks\\r\\n\\r\\n- First run: one real report saved; no invented baseline, score delta, or repaired finding.\\r\\n- Second run: original report unchanged; carry IDs forward; resolve only with passing evidence.\\r\\n- Same-second or parallel runs: distinct filenames; neither replaces the other.\\r\\n- Reduced scope: omitted prior findings remain visible as not rechecked; no automatic resolution.\\r\\n- Scope/rubric change: finding transitions may be shown; whole-score improvement is not claimed.\\r\\n- Previously resolved finding fails again: reopened with the original ID and current evidence.\\r\\n- Permission failure or explicit no-save: no false saved confirmation.\\r\\n- Partial report or fixture: never used as a completed score baseline.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"0e0a3e","wall_time_seconds":0.7215926,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2431,"output":"# Routing and cross-runtime compatibility\\r\\n\\r\\nRequired for every audit. Keep checks bounded and read-only. Inspect the runtimes actually used or explicitly targeted for migration; a single-runtime project does not need duplicate installations to pass. Treat a potential future migration as an optional opportunity unless requested. Never install, synchronize, rename, or run project workflows merely to audit them.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 1. Establish the comparison contract\\r\\n\\r\\n- Read applicable manuals, scoped overrides, bridge documentation, and sync configuration. Identify the canonical source, generated copies, intentional independent implementations, and declared sharing rules. Do not assume the newest file or the Claude copy is authoritative.\\r\\n- Inventory actual project skill locations, including `.claude/skills`, `.agents/skills`, and `.codex/skills` when present or referenced. A literal `.agent/skills` directory is a candidate typo/custom location, not automatically a discovered skill root. Verify it against the installed runtime.\\r\\n- Include relevant user-level roots, linked directories, runtime overrides, and duplicate names when available through discovery/configuration. Do not crawl unrelated personal files or expose configuration secrets. If user-level discovery is unavailable, report that limit.\\r\\n- Establish discovery and precedence using the installed runtime\'s read-only listing/configuration, or current official documentation if needed. Folder presence alone proves neither availability nor precedence. Record the runtime/version when available. Do not assume every listed location works in every version.\\r\\n- Report scope numerically: manuals read, roots inventoried, skills compared, skills whose discovery was checked, resources checked, and workflows exercised. State what was sampled or omitted. A metadata check is not an execution test.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 2. Compare operating instructions\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Compare root `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` when present, then sampled scoped overrides. Compare purpose, user preferences, source authority, routes, output locations, permissions, and workflow instructions.\\r\\n2. Classify each meaningful difference as shared guidance that agrees, an intentional runtime adaptation, a confirmed conflict/omission, or an unresolved difference. Whitespace and line endings are not behavioral conflicts. Enforce exact synchronization only when the project explicitly promises it; report harmless formatting drift as such.\\r\\n3. A missing manual is a migration defect only if the target runtime consequently lacks required guidance and no verified bridge/equivalent supplies it. Do not demand duplicate manuals from a single-runtime setup.\\r\\n4. Show both file/line references, the differing rules, the runtime affected, and the likely behavior. For omissions, cite the source rule plus the inspected destination section/file. Never invent a destination line for absent text.\\r\\n5. Check that bridges preserve shared instructions without pretending unsupported runtime-specific fields or tools are enforced. Different model/tool names may be valid adaptations; losing an approval boundary or source route is substantive.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 3. Check routes in both directions\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Route to target:** resolve concrete paths from the correct base (repository, containing file, declared working directory, user home, or separate host). Verify existence and intended destination. Distinguish concrete references from placeholders, examples, globs, URLs, and remote-host paths. A remote path unavailable locally is unverified, not automatically broken.\\r\\n- **Important target to route:** compare the relevant indexes/manuals with immediate project, knowledge, and skill inventories. Follow index chains; a nested project can be properly routed without a direct root-manual link. Identify useful active material with no usable entry route, using evidence of its relevance. An unclassified folder is a candidate for review, not automatically an orphan.\\r\\n- Check obsolete usernames, moved folders, platform-specific absolute paths, wrong folder spelling/case where material, stale aliases/counts, archived material shown as active, and links pointing at the wrong valid destination. An existing path can still be wrong.\\r\\n- Do not flag every unlinked file. Scratch, generated files, dependencies, archives, and intentionally private/specialist material may be correctly excluded. Distinguish stale factual content from an old file whose contents remain accurate.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 4. Compare skill packages and actual availability\\r\\n\\r\\nMatch skills by declared identity, purpose, and documented aliases, not folder name alone. Inventory broadly by metadata, then inspect required resources for the selected workflows and any suspected mismatch.\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Presence:** required source/target copy or intentional runtime-specific scope.\\r\\n2. **Content:** shared workflow steps, inputs, outputs, boundaries, and behavior. For declared mirrors, compare after only documented transforms. Never blanket-strip runtime text or path differences to manufacture a match.\\r\\n3. **Resources:** required scripts, templates, examples, assets, dependency instructions, and agent references resolve from the target environment. Include extra obsolete mirror files as potential drift; a source-to-target-only comparison misses these. Broken symlinks and targets outside the checkout need explicit evidence.\\r\\n4. **Discovery:** distinguish present on disk, runtime-listed, disabled, and not checked. Detect duplicate identities across project/user roots and determine effective resolution where supported. Report ambiguity if precedence cannot be verified. Do not assume presence in a menu means successful execution.\\r\\n5. **Behavior:** check incompatible tool calls, unsupported instructions, wrong working directories, platform commands, missing environment-variable names without revealing values, and undocumented dependencies. Use safe existing evidence for actual execution; leave paid, posting, or other side-effecting workflows untested in a read-only audit.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 5. Communicate the result\\r\\n\\r\\nUse these finding classes:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Confirmed defect:** evidence shows a missing required route/resource, conflicting instruction, broken path, or unavailable required skill.\\r\\n- **Verification gap:** discovery, remote access, intent, or execution was not established. State the smallest check that would resolve it.\\r\\n- **Intentional difference:** a documented and appropriate runtime adaptation. No repair needed.\\r\\n- **Improvement opportunity:** a useful simplification or extension without a demonstrated failure. Explain the benefit without calling it broken.\\r\\n\\r\\nShow a compact compatibility matrix, using actual paths/runtime names:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Item | Claude evidence | Codex evidence | Finding / ID | Practical consequence |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Shared operating guidance | File/section | File/section or verified bridge | Agrees / intentional difference / conflict / not checked | What changes for the user |\\r\\n| Sampled skill | Canonical/package status | Mirror/discovery status | Equivalent / missing / drift / disabled / not checked | What works or fails after switching |\\r\\n| Required resource or route | Resolved source | Resolved target | Valid / broken / unlinked / not checked | Effect on retrieval or execution |\\r\\n\\r\\nFollow with only actionable findings, grouped by class:\\r\\n\\r\\n| ID / class / priority | Evidence and affected runtime | User impact | Proposed change or verification | Completion check |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Stable ID; repair, verify, or opportunity | Exact file/line pairs or dated runtime result | Concrete failure or uncertainty | Canonical edit/sync/route addition/read-only check | Observable result |\\r\\n\\r\\nPrioritize wrong answers, lost instructions, unavailable workflows, and unsafe permission drift over cosmetic differences. Use one ID for an underlying issue across manual, routing, and skill sections. Do not hide a confirmed lower-priority defect because only three actions are recommended.\\r\\n\\r\\nConclude **blocked in the sampled migration** only for a confirmed defect that prevents a required migrated behavior; **needs verification** for unresolved evidence or a nonblocking confirmed mismatch; **passed inspected checks** when the stated checks passed without unresolved applicable findings. Optional opportunities do not block a pass. Always name unchecked execution/coverage. No fifth score, no migration percentage, no score bonus for duplicate files. No automatic repairs.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Calibration cases\\r\\n\\r\\n- Manuals share all rules but use different supported tool names: intentional adaptation, no conflict.\\r\\n- Project explicitly promises identical manuals; only newline encoding differs: formatting-only, not a behavioral blocker.\\r\\n- A shared permission rule exists only in the source manual, with no target bridge: confirmed omission; cite the source and inspected destination.\\r\\n- A single-runtime project has one manual and skill tree: no missing-copy penalty.\\r\\n- A requested target runtime lists a same-name skill through a verified user-level installation: not missing solely because its project mirror is absent; inspect the effective package.\\r\\n- A mirror matches the documented path rewrite but lacks a required template: confirmed package defect despite matching SKILL.md.\\r\\n- Duplicate project/user skills exist but effective precedence is unknown: ambiguity/verification gap, not an assumed overwrite.\\r\\n- A referenced remote host is unavailable during inspection: verification gap, not a broken local path.\\r\\n- A relevant project is reachable through a domain index: routed; scratch with no incoming link is not a defect.\\r\\n- A skill is listed and enabled but was not run: discovery verified, execution unverified.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"f0ca17","wall_time_seconds":0.5703966,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":644,"output":"---\\r\\naudit_kind: aios\\r\\nhistory_schema: 1\\r\\nrun_id: \\"<UTC timestamp and random suffix>\\"\\r\\nstarted_at: \\"<ISO-8601 UTC>\\"\\r\\ncompleted_at: \\"<ISO-8601 UTC>\\"\\r\\nproject_root: \\"<absolute audited root; use forward slashes in YAML paths>\\"\\r\\nproject_identity: \\"<verified project identity, without credentials>\\"\\r\\nreport_status: complete\\r\\nrubric_version: v2\\r\\nprevious_reports: []\\r\\nscore_baseline: null\\r\\nscores_comparable: false\\r\\nscores:\\r\\n  context: null\\r\\n  connections: null\\r\\n  capabilities: null\\r\\n  cadence: null\\r\\n  raw_total: null\\r\\n  final: null\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# AIOS Audit: <project and date>\\r\\n\\r\\n## Conclusion and scope\\r\\n\\r\\n<What works, most consequential mismatch, inspected runtimes/workflows/domains/probes, counts, exclusions, and verification limits. Mark partial runs explicitly.>\\r\\n\\r\\n## AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md findings\\r\\n\\r\\n<Exact checked/missing/not-checked manuals, shared guidance comparison, finding IDs and file references, scoped coverage, and whether manuals changed.>\\r\\n\\r\\n## Routing and migration compatibility\\r\\n\\r\\n<Compatibility matrix and unique findings grouped by confirmed defect, verification gap, intentional difference, and improvement opportunity. Include source/target evidence and discovery versus execution coverage.>\\r\\n\\r\\n## Routing probes\\r\\n\\r\\n<Five selected questions, attempted routes, sources, and direct/fallback/unresolved results.>\\r\\n\\r\\n## What works\\r\\n\\r\\n<Up to three evidenced strengths.>\\r\\n\\r\\n## Verified operational reliability\\r\\n\\r\\n<All 20 criterion IDs and awarded points, four subtotals, raw total, caps with reasons, final score, and stage. Distinguish defects from unverified evidence. This measures verified operational reliability, not overall usefulness. Partial/unscored entries remain null.>\\r\\n\\r\\n## Progress since the previous audit\\r\\n\\r\\n<Clickable previous-report links and score baseline, or first recorded baseline. Show transitions, actual repairs versus evidence-only gains, regressions, and coverage/rubric changes. Include comparable score arithmetic only when justified.>\\r\\n\\r\\n## Finding ledger\\r\\n\\r\\n| ID | Class | Target / runtime | First seen | Prior status | Current status | Last verified date / state | Evidence | Completion check |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n\\r\\n<Populate current findings plus all carried findings, including not-rechecked items and compact prior closures. No findings is valid when supported by inspected scope.>\\r\\n\\r\\n## Top improvements and next run\\r\\n\\r\\n<Up to three prioritized repair/verify/optional actions with benefit, finding IDs, and acceptance checks. Include a scoped next-run prompt and unresolved verification limits.>\\r\\n"}'}]

## Tool: exec

text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"Get-ChildItem audits -File | Select-Object Name,LastWriteTime",max_output_tokens:2500}));text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"Get-Content AGENTS.md",max_output_tokens:7500}));text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"Get-Content CLAUDE.md",max_output_tokens:7500}));

This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->\\r\\n# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)\\r\\n\\r\\nThe canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\dev\\\\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Identity and mission\\r\\n\\r\\nYou are **Jay 2**, Joshua\'s executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.\\r\\n\\r\\nStanding duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Routing map\\r\\n\\r\\nPaths are relative to the repository root.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Need | Path |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |\\r\\n| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |\\r\\n| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |\\r\\n| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |\\r\\n| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |\\r\\n| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |\\r\\n| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |\\r\\n| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |\\r\\n| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |\\r\\n| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |\\r\\n| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |\\r\\n| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |\\r\\n| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |\\r\\n| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |\\r\\n| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |\\r\\n| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |\\r\\n| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |\\r\\n| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |\\r\\n| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |\\r\\n| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |\\r\\n| Shared Windows CLI tools for Codex, Claude Code, and Antigravity | `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md` — installed paths, PATH refresh, and usage for `uv`, `yt-dlp`, FFmpeg, and Deno |\\r\\n| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |\\r\\n| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |\\r\\n| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |\\r\\n| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |\\r\\n| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |\\r\\n| Current local work in Claude/Codex cloud | `setup/github-sync/README.md`; private `codex/local-sync/<machine>/<checkout>` branches and generated `.cloud-sync/` metadata/conversation exports. Cloud edits use separate task branches. |\\r\\n| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |\\r\\n| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — intake queue; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies clips into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized, so inbox presence alone does not mean unprocessed |\\r\\n| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |\\r\\n| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |\\r\\n\\r\\nApplication repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. The live system — the final freshness escalation.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Voice\\r\\n\\r\\n- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.\\r\\n- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never \\"delve,\\" \\"tapestry,\\" \\"testament,\\" \\"demystify,\\" \\"boasts,\\" \\"in the realm of,\\" and their kin.\\r\\n- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.\\r\\n- **LSAT teaching:** use project-owned `lsat-u-voice` and `references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md`; this Joshua-selected standard takes precedence over older vendor voice blends.\\r\\n- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft\'s paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.\\r\\n- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don\'t over-hedge.\\r\\n- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Tools, keys, and secrets\\r\\n\\r\\n- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua\'s single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.\\r\\n- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.\\r\\n- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.\\r\\n- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.\\r\\n- **Shared Windows command tools:** use `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` from the Windows user PATH. For versions, locations, how to invoke them, or a stale terminal environment, read `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md`. Check before reinstalling; restart the affected IDE when its inherited PATH is stale.\\r\\n- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.\\r\\n- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.\\r\\n- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.\\r\\n- **Authorized cloud transfer (2026-10-07):** private sync branches also carry portable skills, memory and conversation exports. Joshua explicitly authorized credential transfer; implementation uses the authenticated encrypted package described in `setup/github-sync/README.md`; plaintext secrets and its unlock key stay outside GitHub and Drive. Cloud authentication and native browser sessions still require separate verification.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Skills\\r\\n\\r\\nEverything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.\\r\\n\\r\\nActive in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Skill | Use it when |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua\'s explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |\\r\\n| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |\\r\\n| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |\\r\\n| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |\\r\\n| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |\\r\\n| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |\\r\\n| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |\\r\\n| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |\\r\\n| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |\\r\\n| `accommodations-email` | \\"accommodations email to X\\" (dictation: \\"accommodations scale\\"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Ask until you are.\\r\\n- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua\'s explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.\\r\\n- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.\\r\\n- **Root stays thin.** Don\'t accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.\\r\\n- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.\\r\\n- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.\\r\\n- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don\'t grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.\\r\\n- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.\\r\\n- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.\\r\\n- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.\\r\\n- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU\'s recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua\'s explicit approval.\\r\\n- **Memory = native automemory.** \\"Remember that…\\" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.\\r\\n- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)\\r\\n\\r\\n- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.\\r\\n- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.\\r\\n- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.\\r\\n- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.\\r\\n- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.\\r\\n- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.\\r\\n- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don\'t grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.\\r\\n- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.\\r\\n- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.\\r\\n- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.\\r\\n- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU\'s recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua\'s explicit approval.\\r\\n- **Memory = native automemory.** \\"Remember that…\\" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.\\r\\n- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)\\r\\n\\r\\n- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.\\r\\n- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.\\r\\n- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.\\r\\n- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.\\r\\n- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.\\r\\n- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.\\r\\n"}'}]

All paid upfront — no package-balance model exists.\\r\\n- Standard post-consultation offers are a single session at **$45/hour**, a five-hour package at **$40/hour**, and a ten-hour package at **$35/hour**.\\r\\n- In the prospect-facing standard follow-up, show only those hourly rates. The workflow still validates the underlying total charged by each payment link before scheduling and sending.\\r\\n- Label the single-session link `Schedule here`. Label the five-hour and ten-hour package links `Purchase here`.\\r\\n- Post-consultation emails present all three standard offers with a verified action link for each only when no special arrangement exists. A verified person-specific arrangement replaces the standard menu entirely; never mix bespoke terms with unrelated standard offers.\\r\\n- A special arrangement exists only when Joshua explicitly states it, including an explicit statement preserved in the consultation notes or email thread. Never infer a discount or bespoke offer from financial concerns, fee-waiver status, geography, historical links, or other circumstances.\\r\\n- The standard consultation follow-up time is **3:00 PM Toronto time on the next calendar day, including weekends and statutory holidays**, after confirming the consultation occurred, usable notes exist, no follow-up was already sent, and the prospect has not paid.\\r\\n- Consultation follow-ups are scheduled automatically and send at the standard time unless Joshua stops or changes them beforehand. This approval-by-silence rule applies only to the bounded consultation follow-up workflow.\\r\\n- Only one or two lines in a consultation follow-up are personalized from the notes. They prioritize the prospect\'s goal, test timeline, and central LSAT problem; scores appear only when central, and sensitive details are not repeated merely to demonstrate personalization. The lines must sound specific and human, not generic or AI-written.\\r\\n- Do not include a breathing-room line claiming Joshua waited a couple of days or gave the prospect time to think. The standard follow-up sends the next calendar day, and the email must describe timing truthfully.\\r\\n- If the prospect uses 7Sage and their 7Sage profile URL is not already available, the consultation follow-up asks them to send the URL.\\r\\n- The request must specifically ask for the URL copied from the 7Sage Analytics page. Accept only the canonical `https://7sage.com/u/usr_<id>/review` form as an analytics URL. Drill-builder links, settings pages, and login-token URLs are not substitutes; login-token URLs must be treated as credentials and rejected.\\r\\n- Place the conditional 7Sage Analytics request beside the LSAT U signup step near the end of the consultation follow-up. It is a locked conditional line, not one of the one or two AI-personalized recap lines.\\r\\n- Variant C in `email system/Post-Consultation Follow-Up Template.md` is the canonical consultation follow-up base when no special arrangement exists. Workflow automation may fill its bounded personalization, 7Sage, pricing, and verified-link fields, but should not invent a replacement sales structure.\\r\\n- Remove the application-form step from Variant C. Consultation follow-ups must not send Google Forms or `gformsapp.com` links; intake belongs inside LSAT U during onboarding.\\r\\n- The consultation follow-up includes LSAT U account creation near the end of its next steps using `https://lsatprep.study`. A prospect may sign up before payment because Joshua controls student permissions. Intake, permissions, and teaching access still begin only after confirmed payment.\\r\\n- Send the consultation follow-up inside the existing consultation conversation when a usable Gmail thread exists. Otherwise, start a new message with the fixed subject `Next steps + tutoring options`.\\r\\n- Confirmed payment, not an email reply, triggers onboarding. Standard payments resolve through Stripe for packages and Stripe-linked Cal.com for single sessions; a verified special arrangement uses its stated authority. Cancel any scheduled consultation follow-up if payment arrives before its send time.\\r\\n- Automatic consultation follow-ups fail closed unless the recipient, current prospect state, prices, and price-to-link mapping all verify. Reject placeholders, malformed URLs, form editor links, login-token URLs, self-addressed tests, and one payment link reused across distinct paid tiers without an explicit arrangement. Run validation before scheduling and again before sending.\\r\\n- Verified standard links, all three re-checked live against Stripe/Cal.com on 2026-09-08: single session, `https://cal.com/joshua-jtx5ud/session-link` at $45 for 70 minutes; five-hour package, `https://buy.stripe.com/4gMaEY05F3dc9x13NqdZ61x`, renders \\"5 hours — $200.00\\"; ten-hour package, `https://buy.stripe.com/3cI6oI2dN5lk4cHcjWdZ61O`, renders \\"10 hour reduced — $350.00\\". The ten-hour link is correct; the earlier note that no verified $350 link existed was stale. Historical ten-hour links that charge $500 must still not be used.\\r\\n- Students in the **United States and Canada**.\\r\\n- Also building **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study), the online prep platform; application code and product docs live in the sibling `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` repository. Vault project notes remain separate from application code.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Single-session pricing discrepancy requires verification\\r\\n\\r\\nThe offer above says $45/hour, while the dated 2026-09-08 booking-link check records $45 for 70 minutes. These are different descriptions; this audit on 2026-10-08 did not recheck the live booking configuration or establish a new rate. Do not resolve the difference by importing the old vault\'s pricing. Follow the existing live price-to-link validation before using an offer, and preserve the distinction until the current terms are verified.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Money and booking\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Stripe** — packages; **Cal.com (Stripe-linked)** — individual paid sessions and free consultations; **e-transfer** rare (Canada).\\r\\n- All sessions land on **Google Calendar** and are conducted on **Google Meet**.\\r\\n- Tracking today is mainly the Stripe dashboard. **Gap:** no single per-student view of payments and sessions-used vs purchased — Cal.com answers sessions-used; a mirror view belongs in `students/` as `*.generated.md`, never a Markdown ledger.\\r\\n- **Self-scheduling links are situational.** Send the appropriate booking link, instead of proposing times, when a student has paid for a session but has not scheduled it, needs to reschedule, or has paid for a package with sessions to book. Do not apply this as a blanket rule to every prospect or email.\\r\\n- **Billing is contextual.** Before drafting a billing or payment reply, reconstruct the specific person\'s payment source, purchase type, amount or package, relevant agreement, session history, and timeline from the student file and authoritative live systems. Different students can legitimately have different arrangements. Do not force a generic billing answer onto incomplete facts.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Channels\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Gmail (contact@aspiringattorneys.com)** is the main channel — consultations, student threads, follow-ups. The **LSAT U in-app inbox** (Supabase) is a growing second. WhatsApp/iMessage negligible. Solo operator — no team.\\r\\n- Google Meet generates **full transcripts + notes after every session**, arriving by Gmail, attached to the calendar event, and in Drive — the reliable ingest signal for `students/<name>/`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The weekly time-sucks (ranked)\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Post-session work** — notes, homework, assigning work, study schedules.\\r\\n2. **Session prep** — per-student, analytics-informed (7Sage + platforms). Target state: prep is \\"nearly mindless\\"; the system reviews everything and prepares the session.\\r\\n3. **Pre/post-consultation email workflow** — partly covered by the `consultation-*` skills.\\r\\n4. Conducting sessions — the one part Joshua keeps.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Known gaps\\r\\n\\r\\n- No centralized teaching-materials store (scattered, mostly Google Drive) → `corpus/` is the designated home.\\r\\n- No centralized per-student records → `students/` is the designated home.\\r\\n- No central tutoring-business tracker (students, sessions, homework, follow-up state) → future cadence build; mirrors from backends (live systems are authority, the brain mirrors).\\r\\n\\r\\nVoice for anything a student or prospect reads: `wiki/teaching-voice.md`.\\r\\n"}'}]

This is the navigation and filing guide for that material. It is separate from the [LSAT teaching corpus](../../corpus/index.md), while remaining part of the same second brain.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis hub organizes existing authoritative pages rather than copying their knowledge into another set of pages. The intake rules remain in the [LLM Wiki schema](../../system/schema/llm-wiki.md), and the overall catalog remains [the wiki index](../../wiki/index.md).\\r\\n\\r\\n[Recovered practical methods](migrated-source-methods.md) add the old vault\'s detailed artifact-review, skill-evaluation and evidence-checking recipes, plus its unimplemented annotation idea. [Migration coverage](../../research/obsidian-vault-comparison/2026-10-07/active-migration-coverage.md) maps the original research and root files to current homes and explains exclusions.\\r\\n\\r\\n[Karpathy source compilation](../../wiki/karpathy/index.md) now spans domain source pages and explicit semantic coverage. [Use the teaching setup](karpathy-method.md) for the reviewed rules, project agent, teaching skill and scoped Python execution gate. Full-corpus compilation remains incomplete. The [verified collection handoff](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/HANDOFF-VERIFICATION.md) records missing coverage, attribution limits and corrections to the original completeness claims.\\r\\n\\r\\n[Resumable X collector](x-archive-collector.md) documents the free local script, signed-in persistent Chrome route, evidence ledgers, source-preserving repairs and retrieval limits.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Find the right kind of AI knowledge\\r\\n\\r\\n| Category | What belongs here | Existing home to consult or update |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Claude and Codex workflows | Build/review handoffs, work briefs, tool orchestration, runtime differences | [Claude plus Codex](../claude-plus-codex.md) |\\r\\n| Skills and reusable prompts | Skill evaluation, reusable instructions, activation rules, deciding whether an existing skill already covers the job | [Skill catalog](../../skills/INDEX.md), [declared runtime differences](../../skills/PARITY.md), [operating rules](../../AGENTS.md), and [Three Ms framework](../3ms-framework.md) |\\r\\n| Tools, repositories, and integrations | What a tool could solve, prerequisites, overlaps, costs, and evidence from testing | [Obsidian command center](../obsidian-command-center.md), [Firecrawl methods](../firecrawl.md), or the existing reference for the particular tool |\\r\\n| Tips, insights, and prompting methods | Useful reasoning patterns, context management, evaluating outputs, and methods that survive product changes | [Three Ms framework](../3ms-framework.md), [second-brain architecture](../second-brain-levels.md), and [website planning](../website-design-planning.md) when relevant |\\r\\n| Product updates | Claimed new capabilities, affected runtime/version, official evidence, availability, and practical consequences | Update the existing affected reference. Keep release evidence in its source summary rather than creating an undated universal claim |\\r\\n| Proposed improvements | A concrete gap, expected benefit, alternatives, and a testable next action | [Automation backlog](../automation-backlog.md) for automation proposals; [work ledger](../../wiki/tasks.md) for agreed work |\\r\\n| Cloud, local, and backup setup | What exists in each runtime, snapshot freshness, exclusions, and restore limits | [Local-to-cloud sync setup](../../setup/github-sync/README.md), [backup setup](../../setup/backup/README.md), and [application cloud setup](../cloud-session-setup.md) for its specifically stated application scope |\\r\\n| Graphs and navigation | Retrieval problems, graph use, and source-to-graph provenance | [Second-brain architecture](../second-brain-levels.md), [Graphify bridge](../graphify-obsidian-bridge.md), and [Obsidian command center](../obsidian-command-center.md) |\\r\\n\\r\\nThese links establish where to retrieve and maintain a subject. They do not certify every dated claim on the linked pages as current. Check the affected environment before relying on setup or availability claims.\\r\\n\\r\\nNew durable AI subjects with no existing authoritative home can receive a descriptive page under `references/ai-workspace/`. Create that page only when material exists to support it, then link it here and from the wiki index. Categories are navigation, not empty folders that every clip must fill.\\r\\n\\r\\n## A clip follows one intake path\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Capture:** the intended Web Clipper destination is `raw/inbox/`. Saving a clip does not run ingestion, install a skill, or update a configuration. The browser\'s selected vault/template must actually point there; this document does not change browser settings.\\r\\n2. **Request:** Joshua asks to ingest a source, or explicitly authorizes a batch. Determine whether it concerns AI operations, LSAT teaching, a student, or another domain.\\r\\n3. **Preserve:** check content hashes and prior processing. Copy original evidence into `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/`, verify its bytes, and record provenance. Missing images, transcripts, or inaccessible repository contents remain explicit gaps. Preserve changed generations separately.\\r\\n4. **Summarize:** create or update the source\'s page in `wiki/sources/`, with source identity, dates, archived evidence, claims, limitations, and affected knowledge pages.\\r\\n5. **Compile:** compare the source with current knowledge and the actual setup. Update the owning reference instead of making a parallel truth. AI operating methods stay outside the LSAT corpus. A mixed source can inform both domains through distinct updates and shared evidence links.\\r\\n6. **Connect and check:** update this hub when navigation changes, the complete wiki catalog, reciprocal links where useful, and the dated wiki log. Check links, contradictions, and evidence before reporting completion.\\r\\n7. **Implement when requested:** distinguish saved research from an authorized workspace change. Verify an actual change and record its result. Check cloud publication separately; local completion is not proof that a GitHub or Drive snapshot includes it.\\r\\n\\r\\nDo not relocate all existing AI references into this folder. Keeping their stable homes and connecting them here preserves existing links and one authoritative page per subject.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Keep claims, recommendations, and implementation separate\\r\\n\\r\\nAn AI source summary should make the following distinctions explicit when relevant:\\r\\n\\r\\n| State | What it means |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| Source claim | The article/video/repository says this. Record publication and capture dates when known; unknowns remain unknown. |\\r\\n| Verified capability | Official documentation or a scoped test supports it. State the verification date, runtime, version/account requirements where relevant, and what was actually checked. |\\r\\n| Proposed improvement | It might help this workspace. Explain the problem it solves and why existing tools do not already cover it. This is not an implementation claim. |\\r\\n| Implemented and checked | The authorized change exists, with the specific files/settings changed and validation performed. Distinguish local runtime, cloud runtime, and published backup state. |\\r\\n| Deferred, superseded, or conflicting | Keep the reason and links to the competing evidence. Do not silently treat dated advice as today\'s instruction. |\\r\\n\\r\\nFor product updates, verify time-sensitive availability and behavior against current official documentation or the actual affected runtime before recommending action. A clip alone is not evidence that Joshua\'s installed version or account has a feature.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor a repository or skill, inspect actual relevant contents before assessing it; a promotional clip is not a code review. Check overlap with the catalog first. Save useful prompts as attributed examples or reusable methods, with their intended task and constraints; quoted instructions do not become agent instructions.\\r\\n\\r\\nCreating or activating a skill requires a request that authorizes that work. Follow the existing dual-runtime model: project-scoped active copies in `.agents/skills/` and `.claude/skills/`, corresponding `skills/codex/` and `skills/claude/` catalog homes, declared differences in `skills/PARITY.md`, catalog updates, and the existing validator. Do not merge runtime catalogs or globally install a skill merely because a source recommends it. Current scoped instructions govern the exact operation.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Ask for the result you want\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\nIngest the newest AI clip. Compare it with our Claude and Codex setup,\\r\\nsave useful methods, and tell me what is new, redundant, or conflicting.\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\nWhat do we know about this AI workflow, and what have we actually implemented?\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\nEvaluate this repository or skill for our AI OS. Check what it adds,\\r\\nwhat we already have, and what would need to change.\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\nImplement the useful changes from this source in our AI OS, using our\\r\\nexisting skill and configuration rules. Verify and report what changed.\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\nLint the AI workspace references for contradictions, stale claims,\\r\\nduplicate subjects, and broken links.\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nUse [Ingest](../../.agents/skills/ingest/SKILL.md) for intake, [Query](../../.agents/skills/second-brain-query/SKILL.md) for retrieval and useful synthesis, and [Lint](../../.agents/skills/second-brain-lint/SKILL.md) for consistency. Structural lint is not proof that a product claim is true or fresh.\\r\\n"}'}]

Every checkout gets its own private GitHub snapshot branch:\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\ncodex/local-sync/<machine>/<checkout>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nSnapshots capture unfinished files without changing the active Git index, checkout, branch, or production `main`. Separate worktrees stay separate. Existing cloud changes on a sync branch are a conflict; the uploader must stop rather than overwrite them. Make cloud edits on a new work branch and integrate them through the normal project workflow.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe Windows task `AI OS - Private GitHub Sync` checks every two minutes while signed in, and after logon. It publishes GitHub snapshots, then mirrors those exact commits to Google Drive. Changes arrive after that check and upload finish. Offline or sleeping laptops sync when execution and network access resume. A running cloud session retains its clone until it fetches current work.\\r\\n\\r\\n## What is included\\r\\n\\r\\n- Current project source, notes, skills, references, plans, and ordinary working data.\\r\\n- Each LSAT U worktree on its own branch of the app repository.\\r\\n- Readable Claude Code and Codex conversation exports for the covered roots, under `.cloud-sync/conversations/` in the AI OS snapshot.\\r\\n- Generated workspace metadata and a freshness/status record.\\r\\n- Separate global Claude, Codex and shared skill catalogs, global instructions, covered Claude native memories and a consistent readable Codex memory export under `.cloud-sync/runtime/`.\\r\\n- Joshua-authorized credentials and runtime configuration in an authenticated encrypted package under `.cloud-sync/private/`. Plaintext credentials remain excluded from ordinary files and conversation exports.\\r\\n\\r\\nConversation exports preserve user and assistant prose, compaction context, and useful tool calls/results, with credential redaction. They exclude hidden reasoning and binary attachments. They are files Claude/Codex can read, not native chat sessions in either product\'s sidebar. Source JSONL disappearance removes the generated current-view export. UI archive/delete can leave the source file behind, so UI deletion is not guaranteed to remove an export. Previous Git commits retain history; permanent history erasure is not performed automatically.\\r\\n\\r\\nPlaintext credentials, `.env`, raw archives, native browser sessions, OS keyrings, dependency/build/cache/runtime directories, nested Git metadata, and reparse links are excluded from the readable snapshot. Configured credential files and discovered project `.env` files are included only inside the encrypted package. Files above the configured GitHub size limit are reported. Gitleaks screens the upload snapshot and records exclusions without printing secret values. Private Drive laptop backups continue independently.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Credential access in cloud\\r\\n\\r\\nThe encrypted package uses AES-GCM with a random 256-bit key. Its key stays in the ignored, access-restricted `setup/github-sync/state/private/` directory, outside GitHub and Drive. `cloud-key.env` provides the one-time cloud key configuration; never paste its contents into a chat, commit or handoff. Any cloud environment given this key can decrypt all included credentials. Environment variables in Claude\'s settings are visible to users of that environment; account-level access and environment selection matter.\\r\\n\\r\\nAfter privately provisioning `AI_OS_SYNC_KEY`, run these commands from the AI OS cloud checkout:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\npython -m pip install -r setup/github-sync/requirements.txt\\r\\npython setup/github-sync/credential_bundle.py --decrypt .cloud-sync/private/credentials.enc --destination .cloud-private/credentials\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nDo not print decrypted files, enable shell tracing, commit them, or overwrite native cloud authentication with local auth files. Use the central `.env` and relevant project credentials for the specific tool that needs them. GWS keyring data, expiring login tokens and Windows paths can require cloud authentication or path adaptation; encrypted transport does not prove the service will accept them. Decryption refuses existing destination files, so use a fresh protected directory for a refreshed package.\\r\\n\\r\\nEach repository also carries `.cloud-sync/private/redacted-context.enc` when readable files required redaction. This compressed encrypted package preserves those original visible text files with the same unlock key. Its manifest identifies their logical paths. Large encrypted packages use a small .enc descriptor plus .enc.partNNNN files, each below GitHub’s file-size limit. Keep all parts together; the decryption helper verifies and reassembles them automatically. Decrypt into a fresh ignored private directory and read the original when an explanation, skill or code line was redacted. Keep plaintext credentials out of work branches and PRs. Source session JSONL and hidden reasoning are never added to this package.\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\npython setup/github-sync/credential_bundle.py --decrypt .cloud-sync/private/redacted-context.enc --destination .cloud-private/original-context\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nUse the AI OS copy of `credential_bundle.py` for packages belonging to an app checkout, resolving the package and destination paths explicitly. Git commit time is the snapshot freshness authority; generated metadata stays stable on unchanged files to avoid uploading identical archives every check.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The web and local runtimes differ\\r\\n\\r\\nCloud sessions run on separate machines and cannot become identical to the local Windows process. Exported context is readable; native chat state, plugin installations, machine-bound credentials and connected browser sessions are separate. Cloud work returns through a reviewed task branch/PR and normal local integration; this uploader does not silently pull cloud edits over local unfinished work. Native cloud-only conversations are not automatically downloaded by this local exporter.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor the actual same local session from a browser, [Claude Remote Control](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/remote-control) keeps local tools, files and the conversation available remotely. The local computer and Claude process must keep running.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Google Drive mirror\\r\\n\\r\\nThe private folder [AI OS - Live Workspace Sync](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1z3oXAoKOWNqKjAPO-Txtkm0uEgz88sgY) contains machine and checkout folders. Each checkout has `latest.zip` and `manifest.json`; the archive contains the same filtered project and conversation snapshot as GitHub. Uploads replace the same Drive file and verify its checksum and size. Source deletions disappear from the latest archive. Prior Drive revisions and Git history can retain older content. Existing laptop backups stay separate. No Drive files are deleted or shared by this sync.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Start cloud work from the latest snapshot\\r\\n\\r\\nAttach `vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os` and `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`.
